Privacy Policy

Effective Date: April 2026

This Privacy Policy describes how Neovi Media ("we", "us", "our") processes personal data in connection with the GoAtlas software and related services.

1. Data Controller

Neovi Media
Micro-entreprise (France)
SIRET: 852 949 841 00013
Address: 101 AV CLAUDE BERNARD, 11000 CARCASSONNE, France
Email: contact@getgoatlas.com

2. Data We Process

2.1 License and Account Data

To deliver and secure the GoAtlas software, we may process license and technical data such as license key status, activation metadata, and validation logs.

2.2 Support Communications

If you contact us, we process the information you provide (such as email content and attachments) to answer requests and provide support.

2.3 Payment and Billing Data

Payments are handled by Lemon Squeezy (Merchant of Record). Lemon Squeezy processes billing and payment data under its own privacy policy. We may receive limited transaction metadata (for example order status) necessary for licensing. Lemon Squeezy acts as an independent data controller for payment processing.

3. Purposes and Legal Bases (GDPR)

  • Contract performance: license activation, validation, access to paid features, support delivery.
  • Legitimate interests: fraud prevention, software security, service improvement, abuse detection.
  • Legal obligations: accounting, tax, and compliance records where required by law.

4. Data Sharing

We do not sell your personal data.

We may share limited data with service providers strictly required to operate the service, including:

  • Lemon Squeezy (billing and merchant of record operations)
  • Infrastructure providers strictly required for service operation

We do not use tracking technologies, cookies, or behavioral analytics within the Software.

5. International Transfers

Some providers may process data outside the European Economic Area (including the United States). Where applicable, we rely on recognized safeguards such as Standard Contractual Clauses (SCCs) or equivalent legal mechanisms.

6. Retention

We retain personal data only for as long as necessary for the purposes described above, including legal, accounting, or security requirements.

7. Security

We implement reasonable technical and organizational measures to protect data against unauthorized access, alteration, disclosure, or destruction.

8. Your Rights

Under GDPR, you may have rights to:

  • Access your data
  • Rectify inaccurate data
  • Request erasure
  • Restrict or object to processing
  • Data portability (where applicable)

To exercise your rights, contact: contact@getgoatlas.com

You also have the right to lodge a complaint with your local supervisory authority, including the CNIL in France.

9. Minors

The GoAtlas software is not intended for individuals under 18.

10. Changes

We may update this Privacy Policy from time to time. Continued use of the GoAtlas software after an update means you acknowledge the revised policy.

11. Contact

If you have any questions about this Privacy Policy, contact us at:
contact@getgoatlas.com

Terms and Conditions

Effective Date: April 2026

These Terms and Conditions ("Terms") govern your purchase and use of the GoAtlas software ("Software"), provided by Neovi Media ("we", "us", "our").

By purchasing, downloading, activating, or using the Software, you agree to be bound by these Terms. If you do not agree, you must not use the Software.

1. Legal Information

Neovi Media
Micro-entreprise (France)
SIRET: 852 949 841 00013
Address: 101 AV CLAUDE BERNARD, 11000 CARCASSONNE, France
Email: contact@getgoatlas.com

2. Merchant of Record

All payments are processed by Lemon Squeezy, LLC, acting as the Merchant of Record.

As Merchant of Record, Lemon Squeezy is responsible for:

  • Payment processing
  • Billing and invoicing
  • Handling applicable taxes (including VAT)

Neovi Media is solely responsible for:

  • The Software
  • Its functionality
  • Support and licensing

3. Eligibility

You must be at least 18 years old to purchase the Software.

If you are purchasing on behalf of a company, you confirm that you are authorized to bind that entity.

4. Product Description

GoAtlas is a standalone desktop software application that enables users to create animated map-based visual content.

The Software is licensed, not sold, and is subject to the End-User License Agreement (EULA).

5. Subscription and Billing

5.1 Subscription Plans

GoAtlas is offered under subscription plans (monthly or annual).

Pricing may change at any time and will apply to future billing cycles only.

5.2 Billing and Renewal

Subscriptions are billed in advance and renew automatically.

By subscribing, you authorize recurring payments via Lemon Squeezy.

You may cancel at any time. Access will continue until the end of the current billing period.

6. Right of Withdrawal (EU Consumers)

If you are a consumer located in the European Union, you have a statutory right to withdraw from your purchase within fourteen (14) days without giving any reason.

However, by purchasing GoAtlas and activating your license key, you expressly:

  • Request immediate access to the Software
  • Acknowledge that the Software is digital content not supplied on a tangible medium
  • Agree that your right of withdrawal is waived upon activation

Accordingly, no refunds will be issued after activation, except where required by applicable law.

7. Refund Policy

Refunds are not provided once the Software has been activated.

Before activation, refund requests may be considered in accordance with Lemon Squeezy's policies and applicable law.

This does not affect mandatory consumer rights.

Use of the Software requires:

The license is:

  • Non-transferable

You are responsible for all activity associated with your license.

12. User Content

Open Source Licenses

Generated 2026-05-23 · 345 production components.

Go Atlas incorporates the third-party open-source software listed below. Each component remains under its own license; copyright notices are reproduced as required.

Components requiring particular attention

FFmpeg (ffmpeg-static) — GNU GPL-3.0-or-later

Le binaire FFmpeg distribué est sous GNU GPL-3.0-or-later. Le code source correspondant est disponible sur https://www.ffmpeg.org/download.html et https://github.com/eugeneware/ffmpeg-static . FFmpeg est invoqué comme programme séparé (sous-processus) et n’est pas modifié.

Mapbox GL JS (mapbox-gl) — Mapbox Terms of Service

Proprietary component used under Mapbox's commercial license, valid while the associated Mapbox account is active and compliant with the Mapbox Terms of Service (https://www.mapbox.com/legal/tos/). Mapbox attribution and telemetry must not be removed.

BiRefNet_lite (modèle de détourage / background removal) — MIT

Modèle de segmentation utilisé pour le détourage d’images, exécuté via Transformers.js. Licence MIT (base : ZhengPeng7/BiRefNet) — usage commercial autorisé, mention de copyright requise. Source : https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/BiRefNet_lite

argparse@2.0.1

License: Python-2.0

A. HISTORY OF THE SOFTWARE
==========================

Python was created in the early 1990s by Guido van Rossum at Stichting
Mathematisch Centrum (CWI, see http://www.cwi.nl) in the Netherlands
as a successor of a language called ABC.  Guido remains Python's
principal author, although it includes many contributions from others.

In 1995, Guido continued his work on Python at the Corporation for
National Research Initiatives (CNRI, see http://www.cnri.reston.va.us)
in Reston, Virginia where he released several versions of the
software.

In May 2000, Guido and the Python core development team moved to
BeOpen.com to form the BeOpen PythonLabs team.  In October of the same
year, the PythonLabs team moved to Digital Creations, which became
Zope Corporation.  In 2001, the Python Software Foundation (PSF, see
https://www.python.org/psf/) was formed, a non-profit organization
created specifically to own Python-related Intellectual Property.
Zope Corporation was a sponsoring member of the PSF.

All Python releases are Open Source (see http://www.opensource.org for
the Open Source Definition).  Historically, most, but not all, Python
releases have also been GPL-compatible; the table below summarizes
the various releases.

    Release         Derived     Year        Owner       GPL-
                    from                                compatible? (1)

    0.9.0 thru 1.2              1991-1995   CWI         yes
    1.3 thru 1.5.2  1.2         1995-1999   CNRI        yes
    1.6             1.5.2       2000        CNRI        no
    2.0             1.6         2000        BeOpen.com  no
    1.6.1           1.6         2001        CNRI        yes (2)
    2.1             2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         no
    2.0.1           2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.1           2.1+2.0.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.2           2.1.1       2002        PSF         yes
    2.1.3           2.1.2       2002        PSF         yes
    2.2 and above   2.1.1       2001-now    PSF         yes

Footnotes:

(1) GPL-compatible doesn't mean that we're distributing Python under
    the GPL.  All Python licenses, unlike the GPL, let you distribute
    a modified version without making your changes open source.  The
    GPL-compatible licenses make it possible to combine Python with
    other software that is released under the GPL; the others don't.

(2) According to Richard Stallman, 1.6.1 is not GPL-compatible,
    because its license has a choice of law clause.  According to
    CNRI, however, Stallman's lawyer has told CNRI's lawyer that 1.6.1
    is "not incompatible" with the GPL.

Thanks to the many outside volunteers who have worked under Guido's
direction to make these releases possible.

    Copyright (C) <year>  <name of author>

    This program is free software: you can redistribute it and/or modify
    it under the terms of the GNU General Public License as published by
    the Free Software Foundation, either version 3 of the License, or
    (at your option) any later version.

    This program is distributed in the hope that it will be useful,
    but WITHOUT ANY WARRANTY; without even the implied warranty of
    MERCHANTABILITY or FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE.  See the
    GNU General Public License for more details.

    You should have received a copy of the GNU General Public License
    along with this program.  If not, see <https://www.gnu.org/licenses/>.

Also add information on how to contact you by electronic and paper mail.

  If the program does terminal interaction, make it output a short
notice like this when it starts in an interactive mode:

    <program>  Copyright (C) <year>  <name of author>
    This program comes with ABSOLUTELY NO WARRANTY; for details type `show w'.
    This is free software, and you are welcome to redistribute it
    under certain conditions; type `show c' for details.

The hypothetical commands `show w' and `show c' should show the appropriate
parts of the General Public License.  Of course, your program's commands
might be different; for a GUI interface, you would use an "about box".

  You should also get your employer (if you work as a programmer) or school,
if any, to sign a "copyright disclaimer" for the program, if necessary.
For more information on this, and how to apply and follow the GNU GPL, see
<https://www.gnu.org/licenses/>.

  The GNU General Public License does not permit incorporating your program
into proprietary programs.  If your program is a subroutine library, you
may consider it more useful to permit linking proprietary applications with
the library.  If this is what you want to do, use the GNU Lesser General
Public License instead of this License.  But first, please read
<https://www.gnu.org/licenses/why-not-lgpl.html>.
